Implementation: delivery checkout now offers Use my location on an explicit tap, with permission/error/timeout handling, reported accuracy, an optional Google Maps preview and required pin confirmation before submission. Customers can remove/skip GPS; business/building remains mandatory and floor/desk details use the order note. No continuous tracking, address autocomplete or paid map API added. Privacy notice now explains pin use, Google links and device-local references; legal approval/retention gates remain open.

Tracking: successful placement saves the original lookup reference and short customer number before any payment redirect. Successful lookup locks the short number; Track another order intentionally opens manual entry, and recent-order buttons restore originals. A bounded list of up to ten references from the last thirty days restores tracking after drawer closure, navigation, reload and payment return. It stores no names, contact details or GPS and tolerates blocked/malformed storage. Network errors are visible and stale responses cannot replace a later lookup. Private browsing, cleared site data, other devices and references from visits before this release cannot be recovered automatically.

Directory/configuration: the requested Blend Property Group | OnPoint Building · 2 Loerie is appended once to existing saved directories through an audited, reversible trading-document change; other tenants/settings are preserved. The migration marker prevents re-adding it after an administrator removes it. Existing default entry remains. ## 2026-09-29 - customer password recovery and optional email/SMS two-factor sign-in

Implementation: Brett requested Forgot password and customer account 2FA, explicitly choosing optional enrollment in account settings. Added a padded password recovery/code/new-password flow alongside sign-in and registration, retaining the coffee promotion. Account security offers email or SMS enrollment; verified account email, current password and a code delivered to the selected destination are required before activation. SMS uses a separately verified mobile number, never an unverified order/loyalty contact. Enabled accounts receive a challenge after password validation; only a valid single-use code or saved recovery code creates a session. Eight hashed single-use recovery codes are shown once at enrollment. Turning off/changing method requires current password plus existing factor/recovery proof, and rotates/revokes other sessions. Email password recovery preserves 2FA, invalidates existing sessions and pending challenges, and never automatically signs the user in. Existing accounts remain opted out; guest checkout and staff/admin authentication are unchanged.

Security and delivery: additive SQLite tables hold hashed opaque challenges, keyed code/recovery hashes, purpose/account/state bindings, ten-minute expiry, five-guess limits and durable account/destination send/password limits. In-flight credential/security changes invalidate delivery and recovery fallbacks. Reset HTTP responses use the same message and opaque token for known/unknown emails and schedule provider work after the response to avoid provider timing disclosure. Email uses existing Resend configuration; SMS uses the existing Twilio sender/vault credential. Provider outages never fall back to password-only sign-in; enabled users can use saved recovery codes. New security messages omit marketing artwork. The separate account-security SMS choice does not change marketing/order notification consent or switches.

Testing: TypeScript, production build, all 161 unit/integration checks (14 new account-security cases) and all 116 desktop/mobile browser checks passed. Tests cover email/SMS enrollment, session gating/revocation, wrong/expired/replayed/cross-account/cross-purpose codes, resend limits, reset retention of 2FA, recovery-code single use, provider failure, deferred reset delivery, in-flight password changes, anonymous/cross-origin endpoint rejection, mobile layout and reset/sign-in/settings transitions. Full-suite testing before opening time exposed nine existing order fixtures relying on the real trading clock; three test files now explicitly use open fixture settings, without changing production hours. Initial browser selectors also matched Next's route announcer; selectors were scoped to the account content, then the full 116-check run passed. Visually inspected desktop reset and mobile security screenshots; local evidence in Midpoint Hub/account-security-evidence-2026-09-29 uses isolated fixture identities/provider responses.

## 2026-09-28 - recover verified-email guest order history

Problem and implementation: the customer account query only selected orders with the signed-in user ID. Added history refresh every 15 seconds while visible, refresh on return to the page, and Refresh orders. Failed history loads show an error rather than claiming there are no orders; refresh failures retain previously loaded results. The old screenshot layout is superseded by the preceding deployed redesign; the service worker does not cache account HTML or APIs.

## 2026-09-28 - six-digit staff numbers and guarded Yoco POS reference matching

Implementation: each existing and new order receives a separate, permanent six-digit staff number from a transactional database sequence starting at 100000. Numbers are unique, never reset daily or recycled, and fail explicitly at exhaustion. The staff queue, search and POS dialog show the number; Copy Yoco note copies FOND plus six digits. Original customer display references, long payment/tracking references and public lookup behavior are preserved; the staff number does not grant guest access. Additive migration backfills existing orders once without altering their items, amounts, payment history or lifecycle state.

Implementation: added a separate encrypted Yoco personal-application API credential and super-admin-only connection/settings screen. The read-only Orders API connector retrieves completed POS orders, gathers all pages before linking, matches one exact FOND staff number in the POS note, requires the same ZAR net total with no extra tip, confirmed online payment in FOND, accepted local status, no refunds/returns, a unique POS reference and the configured location where specified. Duplicate/multiple notes, mismatches, invalid responses and provider errors remain unmatched with visible issues. Linking records the Yoco order ID, order number and actor/time audit evidence; it never adds payment records, changes balances, charges/refunds, writes to Yoco or advances kitchen/fulfilment status. Existing manual POS capture remains available. Polling runs about every 30 seconds while the authenticated staff board is open, with a shared database lease, bounded pagination and a 30-day outstanding-order window; closed-app background push is not implemented.

Provider evidence and activation gate: current official Yoco documentation confirms Orders API read access and personal business API keys with business/orders:read. Its payment-method enum contains card, cash, instant_eft, gift_voucher and other, not a manual eft value; its net_amount is the amount changing hands, while gross_amount is before discount. The code therefore requires an administrator to verify that a known restaurant EFT-closed order is returned as other before enabling matching. The connection check reads recent POS order references/classifications to support that comparison. Additional-card/instant-EFT payments are not accepted as prepaid manual closure. Defaults are disabled; key replacement disables matching and clears EFT acceptance; online checkout and POS API environments must agree. The actual restaurant EFT classification has NOT been verified. Official references: https://developer.yoco.com/api-reference/yoco-api/orders/list-orders-v-1-orders-get and https://developer.yoco.com/docs/api/authentication/api-keys.

The first complete browser run passed 76/78; both new settings failures were due to the local test fixture lacking its public origin, so origin validation correctly rejected the browser POST. The fixture now declares its local URL without weakening production origin validation. Focused desktop/mobile POS and settings checks passed 4/4, including six-digit display/copy, denied public short-number lookup, manual fulfilment, anonymous/cross-origin denial and refusal to enable unverified EFT matching. ## 2026-09-28 - persistent, louder staff tablet alerts

Implementation: staff sound now defaults on rather than resetting off at every reload; only a deliberate mute is persisted as off on that device. A six-tone triangle chime lasting about 2.2 seconds replaces the quiet 0.4-second two-tone alert (peak gain 0.65 instead of 0.09). Received orders trigger the chime and repeat every 30 seconds until accepted, including tickets already waiting when the board opens. A separate Test sound action avoids accidentally muting while testing. The board restores audio on return and normal click/keyboard interaction, shows actual audio readiness, and visibly asks for a tap when autoplay is blocked. A mobile tap regression discovered during testing was fixed by restoring on the completed click rather than pointer-down, so changing audio state cannot move a tapped control before the click. Granted notification permission is read from the browser on load/return rather than stored only in React state. Android-compatible service-worker notifications open the staff board when tapped; notification failure is isolated from queue polling and shows its own error. Locking stops reminders. OPERATIONS.md records the tablet setup and remaining device limitations.

## 2026-09-28 - basket- and workload-aware collection estimates

Implementation: replaced the previous slowest-single-item estimate with a server-enforced basket and kitchen-workload calculation. Each menu row retains its editable preparation time; every ordered quantity becomes preparation work, and the work is distributed across an administrator-editable number of parallel item stations before the existing editable 5-8% uncertainty buffer is applied. New customer orders also include the current kitchen queue: unpaid online orders are excluded, received/accepted tickets use their full basket work, preparing tickets use only their estimated remaining work, and workload is distributed across an administrator-editable number of parallel kitchen orders. The customer basket states its item/quantity estimate and any current queue delay. The API recalculates both figures at submission and stores the basket minutes and queue-delay minutes separately, so bypassing the browser cannot understate the order. Staff preparation-delay monitoring uses the order's own basket work rather than charging its earlier queue wait again. Existing orders receive safe additive defaults.

Testing: `git diff --check`, TypeScript validation, all 103 unit/integration tests, the optimized Next.js production build and the complete 60-check Playwright suite passed. Coverage proves that item type changes the estimate, four portions take longer than one while allowing configured parallel work, the uncertainty buffer is retained, a third active order adds queue time when two orders can run in parallel, unpaid online orders do not consume kitchen workload, preparation-stage timing excludes prior queue delay, invalid parallel-capacity settings are rejected, the customer explains queue delay, and increasing the live basket from one to four items visibly increases the displayed collection estimate on desktop and mobile.

Verify FOND run `36394922963` passed for that exact revision: https://github.com/blendproperty/fond/actions/runs/36394922963. Merge: the established authorised direct-main workflow used no separate pull request or merge.

Production inherited safe defaults of two parallel items per order and two parallel kitchen orders until an administrator tunes them to observed kitchen capacity. No provider credential, payment mode, notification switch, user or menu row changed.

## 2026-09-28 - Food Truck 15:30 customer-order cut-off

Implementation: added a separate, administrator-editable Food Truck closing time with an approved initial value of 15:30. Customer Food Truck orders and mixed café/Food Truck baskets use the earlier Food Truck cut-off for both collection and delivery, while café-only orders continue to use the 18:30 kitchen close. Food Truck collection choices remain today-only and include 15:30 as the final possible slot when preparation time permits. The customer menu states the Food Truck closing time; after cut-off its item buttons and checkout submission are disabled with a direct explanation. ## 2026-09-23 - Owner access, named change history and guarded rollback

Implementation: added an `Owner` role between Manager and Super Admin. Owners can run operational settings, review the protected administration log, inspect before/after versions for menu, trading, website-content and customer-message changes, roll back only the latest change to an item, and manage staff or manager accounts. Owners cannot view or change provider credentials, provider switches, shared tablet credentials, Owner accounts or Super Admin accounts. Super Admin retains those boundaries. Change snapshots are written atomically with the underlying edit; stale rollback is refused so an older version cannot overwrite newer work. Provider configuration and passwords are deliberately excluded from reversible snapshots. Existing order lifecycle history remains in the permanent order event log, while security, provider, customer, payment and retry actions remain in the protected activity log without exposed secrets.

Testing: `npm.cmd run typecheck` passed; all 93 unit/integration tests passed, including Owner authentication/privilege limits and reversible menu/settings history; the optimized Next.js production build passed with 40 application routes. The complete 52-check Playwright suite passed on desktop and mobile against an isolated in-memory database. Browser coverage proves the Owner Settings screen exposes team oversight and change history, hides provider credentials and privileged role choices, permits staff/manager creation, refuses Owner/Super Admin creation, and preserves existing customer, admin and staff workflows. `git diff --check` passed. No live account, credential, operational setting, order, payment or production data was changed by these tests.

Commit/push and merge: implementation and its initial evidence entry were committed and pushed directly to `origin/main` as `17c584743f8179532d29879ab45d1f0df95d94b6`; no separate pull request or merge was used. ## 2026-09-22 - staging-first release consolidation and tablet identity

Implementation: consolidated FOND around one verified code revision with isolated staging and production operational data. Successful `main` verification now deploys only to `fond-test.mid-point.co.za`; production uses a separate manual `Promote FOND Production` workflow requiring the full commit SHA and a successful staging deployment for that exact revision. SHA mismatches fail instead of producing a misleading green no-op. Customer and staff manifests now use host-specific TEST names and colours. ## 2026-09-15 - customer app promotion formats

Implementation: Marketing & CMS now offers text banners, image cards with editable text overlays, and dismissible native-dialog popups. Includes raster image upload, button label/category selection, active dates, matching card previews and a working popup preview. Existing draft/publish/restore remains the publication boundary. The customer app refreshes published store content every 60 seconds; only server-selected active promotions appear. At most one popup per browser session (first active popup in list), delayed until outside basket/tracking/confirmation. Close, Escape and backdrop dismissal supported. Browser session storage may persist when browsers restore tabs; without storage, suppression lasts the page lifetime.

Images: new additive promotion_images SQLite table stores uploaded bytes alongside existing backup data. Authenticated bounded multipart upload accepts JPG/PNG/WebP signatures up to 2 MB, refuses SVG/HTML, caps the library at 200 images, and uses random public image URLs with explicit MIME/nosniff headers. No remote-image URL fetching. Removing an image from a draft does not delete its bytes, preserving publication rollback. No new production credentials or volumes required.

Testing: production build and TypeScript passed, 42 unit tests and 26 Playwright desktop/mobile checks passed. New coverage includes schedule timezone normalization, bad formats/categories/image URLs, anonymous upload denial, raster retrieval, rejected SVG, format editor/image upload/preview, popup dismissal/session suppression, basket deferral and category navigation. Marketing messages are not sent by this app; consented contacts can be exported for an approved provider. Finance is an operational gross ledger, not bank settlement/tax invoicing or an automatic refund-issuance service. ## 2026-09-15 — menu modifiers, top/slow-movers report, menu filtering + view switcher

Implementation (three commits, `cc59e08`, `4efaef3`, `12187bd`, all pushed directly to `main` via the GitHub MCP file-write path since this sandbox's local git still has no push credentials for this repo): built on top of the admin/CRM backend and left-nav shell from the previous milestone.

- **Menu modifiers** ("add this / remove this", e.g. "Extra cheese +R15", "No onion" at R0, negative prices allowed for a discount-style removal): `Modifier = {id, name, price}` on `Meal`, persisted as `modifiers_json` on `menu_items` (additive `ALTER TABLE` migration in `src/lib/db.ts`), validated and CRUD'd in `src/lib/menu-store.ts` (`validateModifiers`, `addModifier`, `removeModifier`, and `updateMenuItem`'s existing full-replacement `modifiers` field). New admin routes `POST /api/admin/menu/[id]/modifiers` and `DELETE /api/admin/menu/[id]/modifiers/[modifierId]`; the existing `PATCH /api/admin/menu/[id]` also accepts a full replacement array. `src/lib/menu.ts`'s `CartLine` gained `modifierIds?: string[]`; a new `lineKey()` helper (id + sorted modifierIds) lets the same base item appear twice in one basket with different modifier selections without colliding; `quoteCart()` now validates each line's modifier selection against the live menu and returns `selectedModifiers`/`unitPrice`/`subtotal` per line, still always priced server-side against the DB-backed menu, never trusted from the client.
- **Customer PWA**: meal cards show modifier checkboxes when a meal has any; selecting/deselecting updates the displayed price live; "Add" carries the currently-checked modifiers onto the cart line; the basket drawer and running total now use `quoteCart` output (wrapped in a try/catch for the empty/mid-edit case) instead of the old flat `price × quantity` reduce, which previously ignored modifiers entirely.
- **Staff tablet**: the order queue's line list now resolves and displays each line's selected modifier names alongside the item name and quantity. Staff-entered manual orders (`ManualOrderPanel`) were left without modifier selection UI in this pass — not yet decided whether walk-in orders need it.
- **Admin Reports** (`reports` nav item now live instead of "coming soon"): new `GET /api/admin/reports/top-items`, backed by `getItemSalesStats()` in `menu-store.ts` (sums quantity sold per item id across all non-cancelled orders' `lines_json`). Returns top 10 by units sold and a "slow movers" list (bottom-ranked, restricted to currently-available items so a discontinued item doesn't clutter it). Admin UI (`ReportsAdmin` in `admin-panel.tsx`) shows both lists with one-click "Discount" (prompts for a new Rand price, sets `isSpecial`+`specialPrice`+`specialLabel`) and "Showcase" (marks `isSpecial` with a "Featured" label, no price change) actions, both reusing the existing `PATCH /api/admin/menu/[id]` endpoint. Documented limitation (code comment + on-screen note): only unit counts are tracked, not per-item revenue, because `orders.lines_json` stores the raw requested basket, not a priced-at-time-of-order snapshot — reconstructing revenue per item isn't possible from existing data without a schema change to snapshot prices at order time.
- **Menu admin filtering + views**: `MenuAdmin` gained a text search (name), category filter, availability filter (on-menu / hidden / both), and a "specials only" checkbox, plus a List/Card/Compact view switcher (`ViewMode`, three icon-button toggle in the toolbar) that changes the CSS grid/layout of `MenuRow` without changing its data or behaviour — compact view hides the description line, card view lays items out in a responsive grid instead of a stacked list.

Testing: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm test` — 22/22 unit tests passed (unchanged from before this milestone; no new test files were added for modifiers/reports/filtering — this is a gap, flagged below). `npm run build` — passed, all 21 routes compiled including the two new modifier routes and the new reports route.

Push: three commits pushed directly to `main` (no PR) via `mcp__remote-devices__github__push_files`, following the same reasoning as every prior milestone (no local push credentials). Commit `cc59e08` — lib/API layer (menu.ts, db.ts, menu-store.ts, the menu/[id] route plus the three new modifier/report route files). Commit `4efaef3` — component layer (ordering-app.tsx, staff-tablet.tsx, admin-panel.tsx). Commit `12187bd` — globals.css. Deployment: none yet — these commits have not been deployed to the VPS as of this entry; the live site still runs whatever commit was deployed at the end of the previous milestone.

Open items carried forward from this milestone: no automated tests were written for modifiers (add/remove/validation/pricing with modifiers), the reports aggregation, or the new admin filters — existing 22 tests still pass because they don't touch this new surface area; staff manual-order entry has no modifier UI; per-item revenue in Reports is not tracked (units only, documented above); deployment to the VPS and a live-verification pass are still open, as is everything already open from the prior CRM-backend milestone (customer/marketing CMS, finance, settings sections remain honest "not built yet" placeholders — not touched in this pass). The user's request also asked to "do all the items in the CRM complete everything" — this pass built the three concretely-specified items (modifiers, top/slow movers report, menu filtering+views) and directly related admin plumbing; it did not attempt the full unscoped 18-section enterprise spec from `FOND_Full_Backend_Specification.md`, since most of that still depends on business decisions (staff roles, notification providers, marketing scope, budget) that remain unanswered — flagged plainly here rather than silently declared done.

Current model is guest PWA ordering into FOND's own staff tablet, manual POS capture if needed, payment in person, and no Yoco integration. Admin currently provides menu/specials and order history, not a complete CRM. Individual staff accounts are not implemented.

Implementation: feature/reliable-order-lifecycle adds atomic persistent idempotency for customer/manual orders, browser retry keys and double-submit guards, full-entropy references with a unique index for new references, purchased line snapshots, transactional status audit events, stale-status rejection and server-confirmed tablet updates with visible errors. Staff-only history API records shared-staff-tablet honestly. Health now checks DB access and reports ordering mode. Deployment workflow waits for successful main verification and checks the exact triggering SHA. README reconciled with actual application.

Testing: 26 unit tests passed; Next production build/TypeScript passed; all 10 Playwright desktop/mobile tests passed, including API duplicate/conflict checks, anonymous access rejection, staff/admin separation and lifecycle history. Initial API test failed because Secure cookies are not automatically sent by the HTTP test client; the corrected local test explicitly forwards the server-issued cookie. Docker image fond:reliability built successfully (manifest list sha256:11562fabc0257bbc46ae4a89e82e4f558b8bec430da6cd861d002f9007ca06bf). No Docker runtime or restart/restore test is claimed for this checkpoint.

Commit/push: this checkpoint is included in the feature branch commit; remote presence is verified after push in the task handoff. Merge: not merged. Deployment/configuration: no production changes; workflow execution and migration against a production backup remain unverified. Live production verification: read-only site, hero image and health returned HTTP 200 during the initial review; the live health still reported obsolete preview mode. No live order or message was sent.

Open gates: review/merge and CI evidence; database backup/restore and controlled staff UAT before promotion; named staff roles and access controls; capacity/hours/availability policies; durable notification outbox/retries and provider delivery verification; delivery/payment business rules; customer history/auth design; real-device installation. Historical orders keep old short references and cannot retroactively gain purchased item snapshots or earlier audit events. New retry keys protect unchanged submissions; changed payloads are new requests. Audit history currently has an authenticated API only, no history UI. No complete CRM, automated POS or printer integration is claimed.

## 2026-09-14 — first-party auth and durable order-request store

Provider re-check (gate verification, required before touching src/lib/yoco.ts): searched developer.yoco.com, Yoco's help centre and Yoco's 2026-06-17 "Yoco Next 2026" blog post for any change to the 2026-09-11 finding. Result: no change. developer.yoco.com/api-reference still lists only the read-only Yoco API (fetch orders/payments/payouts) and the Checkout API (create online payment checkouts) — neither accepts a created order for Counter/staff acceptance. The blog's "prep stations" feature is Yoco's own in-house Counter-to-kitchen routing for orders placed inside Counter, not an API for external systems. No printer or order-injection endpoint exists anywhere in the docs, and no partner/marketplace ordering program was found. `src/lib/yoco.ts` therefore still deliberately throws and was not touched. Do not reinterpret this as a green light without a fresh check and a dated source. *(Correction: this finding understated the real API surface — see the two entries below dated later on 2026-09-14. `WebFetch` against developer.yoco.com's rendered docs was silently truncating/summarizing pages and missing whole sidebar sections; browser navigation surfaced far more. Treat single-tool doc research on this site as unreliable going forward — cross-check with an actual browser render before asserting an endpoint doesn't exist.)*

Implementation: added first-party email/password authentication (`src/lib/auth.ts` — scrypt password hashing, 30-day session tokens, HttpOnly cookie `fond_session`) and a durable SQLite store (`src/lib/db.ts`, Node's built-in `node:sqlite`, no native module so the Alpine image needs no compiler stage). New routes: `POST /api/auth/signup`, `POST /api/auth/login`, `POST /api/auth/logout`, `GET /api/auth/session`. `POST /api/orders` durably records the requested basket via `src/lib/order-requests.ts` when the requester is signed in (`GET /api/orders` returns that user's history) — but the HTTP response is unchanged for everyone: still `503 ORDERING_NOT_ENABLED`, still no payment taken, no Yoco call, no kitchen order, because the Yoco gate above remains closed. *(Correction, same day: the version originally described here 401'd anonymous requests before returning that 503, which broke the pre-existing e2e contract that anonymous `POST /api/orders` always gets 503 — see the CI-fix entry below. The fail-closed 503 is unconditional; sign-in only gates whether the request is durably recorded.)* This closes the "no durable order store" and "no authentication" gaps at the infrastructure level without claiming live ordering. The customer-facing UI (`src/components/ordering-app.tsx`) was intentionally left on session-only demo state in this pass — wiring sign-in/sign-up screens and switching the basket flow to the new authenticated endpoints is still open, tracked below. Dockerfile now creates `/app/data` (owned by the existing non-root `fond` UID 1001) and sets `FOND_DB_PATH=/app/data/fond.sqlite`; `compose.yaml` adds a named `fond-data` volume mounted there so the database survives redeploys; `.env.example` documents `FOND_DB_PATH`.

Testing: `npm run typecheck` passed with no errors. `npm test` — 10/10 unit tests passed (7 previous + 3 new files: `tests/auth.test.ts`, `tests/order-requests.test.ts`), all against an in-memory SQLite DB (`FOND_DB_PATH=:memory:`), no state leakage between tests. `npm run build` passed. Manual smoke test against the built standalone server confirmed signup/login/logout/session and the persisted-order-request flow.

Push: opened as pull request #1 (`feature/auth-and-durable-order-requests` → `main`) rather than pushed straight to `main`, written via the GitHub API/MCP file-write path since this session's sandboxed git proxy has no push credentials for this repository (`git push` returns 403 "not in this session's authorized repository set").

Deployment: none yet at this point in the day. Live verification: none yet.

## 2026-09-14 (later) — CI fix, merge, first VPS deployment, and CI/CD

Implementation: PR #1's CI failed on both `push` and `pull_request` runs. Root cause: `POST /api/orders` had been changed to return `401 AUTH_REQUIRED` for an unauthenticated caller before ever reaching the fail-closed response, but `e2e/ordering.spec.ts`'s "PWA manifest and fail-closed order API" test posts unauthenticated and asserts `503`/`ORDERING_NOT_ENABLED` — a real regression, not a flaky test. Fix (commit `a028cb8` on the PR branch): the `503 ORDERING_NOT_ENABLED` response is now unconditional for every caller, authenticated or not, valid basket or not; sign-in only gates the *bonus* behaviour of durably recording the request. Reasoning: the one invariant this endpoint must never violate is claiming a real order happened — that must hold regardless of auth state, so auth has no business gating it.

Testing: reran `npm run typecheck` (clean) and `npm test` (10/10) after the fix. Rebuilt with `npm run build` (all 8 routes compiled). Manually started the built standalone server and replicated both code paths: an anonymous `POST /api/orders` with the e2e test's exact payload (`{"amount":1}`) returned `503`/`ORDERING_NOT_ENABLED` with the original message text (no "saved" suffix) — matching pre-existing behaviour exactly; a signed-in request with a valid basket returned the same `503` with the "Your request has been saved" suffix, and `GET /api/orders` returned it back with correct `totalCents` and `blocked_pending_pos_integration` status. Brett confirmed GitHub Actions CI (`Verify FOND`) went green on the PR after the fix.

Push/merge: PR #1 merged into `main` by Brett via the GitHub UI (`merged_at` 2026-09-14T06:50:13Z, merge commit history: 16 commits, +440/-3 across 15 files). Two follow-up commits (workflow cleanup) landed on the feature branch a few minutes *after* the merge and were therefore not included in it; applied directly to `main` afterward (commit `7e3cab9`) once noticed.

CI/CD: added `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` — on push to `main` (or manual dispatch), SSHes into the VPS using three repository secrets Brett added himself in GitHub's Settings → Secrets UI (`VPS_HOST`, `VPS_USER`, `VPS_SSH_KEY` — a dedicated `github-actions-deploy` keypair Brett generated directly on the VPS; this session never generated, saw, or handled the private key, per a hard tool-level restriction on materializing credentials), then `git fetch`/`checkout main`/`reset --hard origin/main` and `docker compose up -d --build` inside the existing `/opt/fond` checkout. First run ("Deploy FOND #1", commit `7e3cab9`) succeeded in 39s per GitHub Actions' own run history — the SSH connection, git update and rebuild all worked end-to-end.

Deployment (manual, same session, before CI/CD existed): built and ran the `feature/auth-and-durable-order-requests` branch directly on the Hostinger VPS (93.127.186.194, `srv938083`) via commands relayed through Brett's own root SSH session (this session's device-bridge shell access was unavailable — broken by an unrelated Windows update — so every command was given to Brett to paste rather than run directly). First attempt on port 8090 via bare `docker run` worked internally (`/api/health` returned healthy over localhost) but was unreachable externally — Hostinger's own cloud firewall (separate from the box's `ufw`) was silently dropping the port. Switched to routing through the VPS's existing shared Traefik instance instead of fighting per-port firewall rules: added a DNS-only (non-Cloudflare-proxied) `fond.mid-point.co.za` A record to 93.127.186.194 as an explicitly-agreed staging subdomain (a dedicated domain to follow later), confirmed the existing cert resolver name (`mytlschallenge`, TLS-ALPN-01 — confirming DNS-only was required, since Cloudflare's proxy would intercept the TLS-ALPN challenge) and entrypoint (`websecure`) by grepping other projects' compose files on the same VPS, set `.env` (`FOND_HOST=fond.mid-point.co.za`, `TRAEFIK_NETWORK=root_default`, `TRAEFIK_ENTRYPOINT=websecure`, `TRAEFIK_CERTRESOLVER=mytlschallenge`, `FOND_DB_PATH=/app/data/fond.sqlite`), then ran `docker compose up -d --build`.

Live verification: `https://fond.mid-point.co.za/api/health` returned `{"status":"ok","application":"fond","mode":"preview","liveOrdering":false}` — confirmed directly by Brett in his own browser (this sandboxed session's own network egress is allowlisted and cannot reach that domain to check it itself). Real TLS certificate issued via `mytlschallenge`. This is the first live, externally-reachable deployment of FOND. It remains explicitly a staging subdomain on the existing `mid-point.co.za` domain, not the final approved FOND hostname.

Open gates (updated 2026-09-14, end of day): Yoco human confirmation and one real incoming POS order accepted and printed once (unchanged, still closed — re-checked earlier today, no provider change); customer-facing sign-up/login UI and wiring the basket flow to the authenticated endpoints (backend ready since this morning, UI still untouched); idempotency and payment reconciliation/webhook verification (nothing to reconcile against without a payment/POS connection); cancellations/refunds; ordering capacity/hours; approved FOND menu/prices/allergens/CI/photography; real-device installation and staff UAT; a dedicated production hostname (currently a `mid-point.co.za` staging subdomain, explicitly agreed as temporary); Playwright browser suite has not been re-run against the deployed build specifically (CI covers it on every push, most recently green); no `noindex`/basic-auth added yet to keep the staging subdomain out of search/crawl before it's ready for real customers — flagged to Brett, not yet actioned. No live payments or kitchen orders enabled. No Asana scope.

## 2026-09-14 (later still) — Yoco domain-verification block found; corrected API research begins

Yoco Checkout API domain verification: Brett's Yoco "Verified Domains" panel shows `fond.mid-point.co.za` as "Unable to review — it looks like your website is unavailable or still being built." No documented technical remedy exists (no meta tag/file/DNS record described anywhere in Yoco's docs or help centre); this reads as a manual/business review gate, most likely triggered by the site currently being a bare staging preview with provisional menu/prices/branding and no real business content, T&Cs or contact info. This does not block building or testing the Checkout API integration against Yoco's test/sandbox keys — it only blocks switching to live keys for real customer payments via Checkout API specifically. Revisit once the site has real menu/branding content worth a human reviewer seeing. *(See the entry below: Payment Links may sidestep this block entirely since they don't involve a merchant-hosted redirect domain.)*

Correction to prior API-surface findings (2026-09-11 and 2026-09-14 morning entries above): those findings were wrong about the extent of Yoco's public API and were produced by fetching developer.yoco.com through a single automated tool that was silently missing whole sections of the rendered docs site (it summarized pages down to "only two APIs exist" when the live site's own sidebar lists far more). Re-verified by driving an actual browser against developer.yoco.com/api-reference and reading its full rendered navigation tree directly. Confirmed real, currently-documented endpoints beyond what was previously found:

- **Catalogue is a real, documented, read-only API** — `GET /v1/items`, `GET /v1/items/:item_id`, plus Item Brands and Item Categories (fetch + list, cursor-paginated, `created_at`/`updated_at` range filters), scope `business/catalogue:read` (deprecated alias `business/products:read`, still accepted). Response includes name, description, `default_price` (amount/currency), barcode, tax, images, variants, modifier groups. **This means FOND's menu can be pulled live from Yoco's own product catalogue** (maintained via the Yoco app or CSV/bulk upload, since there is still no documented *write* endpoint for items/categories/brands) rather than hand-maintained in `src/lib/menu.ts`. Matching webhook events exist too: Item Created/Updated/Deleted, Item Category Created/Updated/Deleted, so a sync can be push-based rather than polled.
- **Orders API is real but still read-only** — `GET /v1/orders`, `GET /v1/orders/:order_id`; an order has state `open`/`completed`/`cancelled`. Webhook events exist for Order Created/Updated/Completed/Cancelled (thin payload: just `business_id`/`order_id`/`event_type` — the full order must be fetched separately). This lets FOND *observe* orders that already exist in Yoco's system in real time, but there is still no POST/create endpoint in the public reference — nothing lets an external system inject a new order with line items for Counter/staff to see. The original core finding stands: **no self-serve API exists for FOND to place an order into Yoco Counter or trigger prep-station/kitchen printing.**
- **New: Web POS API** — `POST /v1/webpos` registers a virtual "device" (e.g. a named station), then `POST /v1/webpos/:webpos_device_id/payments` (scopes `business/webpos:read`+`:write`) initiates a **real, in-person card payment on Brett's actual physical Yoco card machine** — the response includes the real terminal model/serial once a physical terminal picks it up, and status resolves to `successful`/`failed`/`pending`. Payload is amount + a free-text `client_reference`/`metadata` only — no line items — and it requires the customer to be physically present to tap/insert a card on that terminal, so it fits an in-store "order on a tablet at the counter, then tap the card machine" flow, not a remote pay-ahead-from-home flow.
- Also newly confirmed to exist (not yet evaluated for relevance): Locations, Modifier groups, Staff, Card machines (fetch/list existing physical terminals), Capital (merchant cash-advance data), and full OAuth 2.0 token/userinfo/logout endpoints for Yoco Connect-style third-party auth.

Net effect on architecture: the "customer PWA → FOND backend → staff accept → printer" redesign (Yoco used only for payment, FOND owns the order/kitchen state) remains the correct plan. What changes: (1) FOND's menu/pricing can now be sourced live from Yoco's real catalogue API if Brett wants menu management to happen inside the Yoco app; (2) Web POS is a second, real, hardware-integrated payment option worth considering for in-store ordering specifically, alongside Checkout API for remote ordering.

Process note: going forward, treat any "no such Yoco endpoint exists" conclusion as unverified until confirmed by an actual browser render of developer.yoco.com, not by `WebFetch` alone — `WebFetch` against this specific site has now twice produced confidently wrong negative findings.

## 2026-09-14 (full API surface review) — complete pass over every Yoco endpoint relevant to FOND

Following a direct request to review the entire API reference rather than a sample, every endpoint group in the live sidebar was opened and read (not just listed): OAuth API (introduction, authorize/token/userinfo/logout, token-info), and under the Yoco API — Capital, Card machines, Items/Item Brands/Item Categories, Locations, Modifier groups, Orders (incl. full `Order` schema), Payment Links, Payments, Payouts, Refunds, Staff, Web POS, Webhooks (management + all 12 event-type payloads), plus the Checkout API's Checkouts and Webhook Events sections, plus the `/docs` getting-started and security pages. Findings that materially affect FOND:

- **Authentication model, clarified.** Brett (as the business owner) does not need Yoco's partner/OAuth approval process to use any of the self-serve `business/*` scopes. `developer.yoco.com/docs/getting-started/build-with-yoco/yoco-api/personal-applications` documents a direct path: log in to the Yoco Developer Console, create a "personal application", pick only the scopes needed, generate a static API key immediately (shown once). OAuth 2.0 (Authorization Code flow) exists as a separate mechanism only for third-party apps that need to access *other* merchants' Yoco accounts on their behalf (e.g. a SaaS product serving many independent restaurants) — not needed for FOND acting on Brett's own single business. So catalogue sync, orders/payments visibility, webhooks, and Web POS are all available today with a personal-application API key; only *order injection under an external channel* (the Uber-Eats-style mechanism, see below) requires the separate Partnership application.
- **The full `Order` object confirms the actual mechanism behind Uber-Eats-style integrations.** `GET /v1/orders/:id` returns (among other fields) `channel` (free-form string, Yoco's own example value is literally `"uber_eats"`) and `external_id` (the originating system's own order ID, for reconciliation), plus a full `line_items` array (name, quantity, unit price, modifiers, tax, discounts) and a `payments` array showing each payment's `payment_source` (seen values: `payment_link`; `checkout`, `card_machine`, `webpos` are the other plausible enum members though not enumerated on this page) and `card_machine_id`. This proves external systems can and do inject full itemised orders into a merchant's Yoco account (that's how a Deliveroo/Uber-Eats order appears on Counter and prints) — but `developer.yoco.com/partnerships` confirms this capability is only unlocked via Yoco's formal Partner application + an approved OAuth application, not available through a personal-application API key. This is a real, viable path for FOND, just a business-development one (apply, wait for approval) rather than an immediate technical build.
- **Payment Links may be the practical way to accept real payments today, bypassing the domain-verification block.** `POST /v1/payment_links/` (scopes `business/orders:read`+`:write`) takes just an `amount` and a `customer_reference`/`customer_description` and returns a `pay.yoco.com/r/...` URL with status `pending`/`paid`/`cancelled` — no `successUrl`/`cancelUrl`/redirect domain involved at all, unlike Checkout API. Since the Verified-Domains gate that's currently blocking Checkout API live keys is specifically about a merchant-hosted redirect domain, Payment Links likely don't require it (not yet confirmed with Yoco support — flagged as an assumption to verify before relying on it). If confirmed, FOND could: build the order in its own DB → call this endpoint for the total → send the customer the returned pay.yoco.com link (e.g. via the order confirmation screen or SMS/WhatsApp) → poll or webhook (`payment.created`/order webhooks) to detect payment → release the order to staff. This is a real, buildable stand-in for Checkout API while the domain review is stuck, at the cost of a slightly clunkier customer experience (redirected to a generic Yoco-hosted link page rather than an embedded/branded checkout).
- **Checkout API's own schema supports itemisation for display.** `POST /api/checkouts` (`https://payments.yoco.com/api/checkouts`) accepts optional `lineItems`, `totalDiscount`, `totalTaxAmount`, `subtotalAmount`, `metadata`, `externalId` and `clientReferenceId` alongside the required `amount`/`currency` — so once live keys are unlocked, FOND's actual basket can be shown to the customer on Yoco's payment page and reconciled back via `externalId`, not just a bare total. Also supports an `Idempotency-Key` header to prevent duplicate checkout creation on retry — should be used when FOND eventually wires this up. The `payment.succeeded` webhook payload was also confirmed (nested `payload.metadata.checkoutId`, masked card details) — matches the webhook-verification design already planned.
- **Refunds, Payouts, Payments** are all straightforward read-only ledger data (`business/orders:read` scope covers payments/refunds; each references `staff_id`, `order_id`, `payment_id`) — useful later for a reconciliation/reporting view, not needed for the current build.
- **Staff API** (`business/staff:read`) lists Yoco's own staff records (name, role, active/inactive, staff number) — could be used later to map "who accepted this order in FOND" to a real Yoco staff member for reporting, but FOND's own accept-flow doesn't need this to function.
- **Locations, Modifier groups, Card machines** are read-only reference data (multi-location support, item add-on/modifier definitions matching what `line_items[].modifiers` can carry, and the merchant's registered physical terminals respectively) — none block or unlock anything for FOND's core flow; Modifier groups could be worth syncing alongside Items later if FOND ever needs "add cheese"-style options to match Yoco's own catalogue.
- **Webhook subscriptions are self-serve too** — `POST /v1/webhooks/subscriptions/` (scopes `application/webhooks:read`+`:write`) takes a `notification_url` and a list of event types and returns a signing `secret` shown once, standard HMAC-verification pattern, matching what's already planned for the Checkout API webhook.

Updated recommendation: (1) generate a personal-application API key in Brett's Yoco Developer Console scoped to `business/catalogue:read`, `business/orders:read`, `business/webpos:read`+`:write`, `application/webhooks:read`+`:write` — no approval wait, usable immediately; (2) verify with Yoco support (or by testing in sandbox) whether Payment Links genuinely bypass Verified-Domains, and if so use them as the near-term live-payment path instead of waiting on Checkout API's domain review; (3) submit the Yoco Partnership application in parallel as the real long-term fix for order-injection/kitchen-printing (channel-based orders), while building FOND's own staff-accept-and-print flow now as the thing that works regardless of that application's outcome; (4) still do not touch `src/lib/yoco.ts`'s throwing behaviour until one of these payment paths is actually implemented and tested end-to-end.

## 2026-09-14 (evening) — architectural pivot: Yoco dropped entirely, real menu, facility staff tablet

Decision (Brett, verbatim): "We have decided to kill the Yoco integration and rather go with a PWA tablet at the facility they can add orders manually to system as needed etc." All prior Yoco research above (provider gates, API surface review, Partnership path) is now historical context only — nothing in it is being pursued. Payment happens entirely outside the app from now on: staff take payment in person on the existing card machine or cash at collection; FOND's job is only to track what was ordered and its kitchen/collection status.

Branding decision (Brett, via direct question after reviewing the uploaded `Midpoint_Corporate_Identity_v1.0.pdf`): the CI doc shows FOND is architecturally slated to become "Midpoint Cafe" but flags that rename as explicitly open/unbudgeted; the two uploaded menu PDFs (`FOND Midrand_Main Menu_V4.pdf`, `FOND Midrand_Tapas Menu_V3.pdf`) and current live signage still say "FOND". Offered three options (keep FOND as-is / switch to Midpoint Cafe now / keep FOND name+menu but adopt Midpoint's confirmed design system); Brett chose the third: **FOND name and menu content stay, but the visual system now follows Midpoint's Corporate Identity v1.0 exactly** — palette Ink `#082121` / Signal Aqua `#39EAE6` / Mist `#F3F7F6` / Slate `#6D7280` / Line `#CACDD5` / Paper `#FFFFFF`, plus FOND's own carried-over venue accent Oxblood `#8C3126` / Bone `#EFE7DA`; typeface Figtree (300–800) with documented OS fallbacks; Aqua confined to text/labels on dark Ink backgrounds only per the CI doc's WCAG table (Aqua-on-Paper/Mist "FAILS BADLY" at 1.49:1 and must never be used).

Implementation:
- **Yoco removed entirely**: deleted `src/lib/yoco.ts` (`YocoOrderGateway`), `src/lib/order-requests.ts`, and their test file. No payment provider code remains in the app.
- **Real menu**: `src/lib/menu.ts` rewritten from the two uploaded PDFs — 15 categories (All-Day Breakfast, The Grill, Plates, Burgers, Sandwiches, Salads, Desserts, Pizzas & Foldovers, Tapas, Coffee, Tea & Steamers, Cold Brew & Iced, Smoothies, Cold Bar & Juice, Sides/Sauces/Add-Ons), 100+ real items with real Rand prices and diet tags (vegan/vegetarian/gluten-free), replacing all placeholder demo data.
- **Real order lifecycle**: new `src/lib/orders.ts` and `orders` SQLite table (replacing `order_requests`). States `received → accepted → ready → completed`, with `cancelled` reachable from any non-terminal state; transitions enforced server-side via an explicit map, rejecting invalid moves with a typed `OrderTransitionError`. Two sources: `customer` (public PWA, starts at `received`) and `staff` (typed into the facility tablet for a walk-in/phone order already taken, starts at `accepted`).
- **`POST /api/orders` now really creates orders** (201, no more fail-closed 503) and **`GET /api/orders?reference=`** is a new anonymous status lookup — customer ordering no longer requires an account; a name + optional note is captured directly on the order.
- **Facility staff tablet** (`/staff`, `src/components/staff-tablet.tsx`): a single shared access code (`FOND_STAFF_CODE` env var) gates the whole device via an HMAC-based cookie token (`src/lib/staff-auth.ts`) that never stores the raw code — appropriate for one shared physical device rather than per-staff logins. Staff see a 3-column live queue (New/Preparing/Ready), can advance or cancel any order, and can key in a manual order (category-tabbed item grid, same menu data) for walk-ins/phone orders. New routes: `POST /api/staff/login`, `POST /api/staff/logout`, `GET`/`POST /api/staff/orders`, `PATCH /api/staff/orders/[id]`.
- **Customer PWA** (`src/components/ordering-app.tsx`) rewritten: removed all "preview only"/Yoco-pending copy, added name/note fields to checkout, real `fetch('/api/orders', ...)` on submit, replaced the old session-only "My previews" drawer with a "Track order" drawer backed by the new reference-lookup endpoint, confirmation copy now reads "Order sent to FOND" / "Please pay at the counter on collection."
- **Styling** (`src/app/globals.css`, `src/app/layout.tsx` theme colour): rewritten to Midpoint's design-system tokens end-to-end, including a new `.staff-*` block for the facility tablet UI, careful never to place Aqua as text on Paper/Mist per the CI doc's contrast table.
- `.env.example` updated: removed the Yoco-secrets note, documented `FOND_STAFF_CODE`.

Testing: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm test` — 13/13 unit tests passed (existing `auth.test.ts` unchanged; `cart.test.ts` updated to quote real menu item ids; two new files `orders.test.ts` — order creation/status defaults by source, input validation, reference lookup, active-order filtering, valid/invalid status transitions — and `staff-auth.test.ts` — correct/incorrect code, token validation against missing/forged tokens — all against an in-memory SQLite DB). `npm run build` — passed, all 14 routes compiled (new `/staff`, `/api/staff/*` routes present, old order-request routes gone). `e2e/ordering.spec.ts` rewritten for the new flow (real order placement + tracking, updated PWA/API contract assertions, new staff-tablet unlock test) and run locally with Playwright against the production build — **4/4 passed**, confirming the customer order → confirmation → track flow, the PWA manifest/asset contract, offline fallback, and staff-tablet code-gate all work end-to-end.

## 2026-09-14 (night) — PR #2 CI fix, real header photo, and a discovered tool limitation

Implementation: opened the above pivot as pull request #2 (`feature/kill-yoco-staff-tablet` → `main`) via the GitHub API/MCP file-write path (same reason as always — this sandbox's local git has no push credentials for `blendproperty/fond`). Initial CI run failed both `pull_request` and `push` checks ("All checks have failed"). Root cause: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `test:e2e` step didn't set `FOND_STAFF_CODE`/`FOND_DB_PATH`, so the new staff-tablet e2e test's login POST hit `checkStaffCode`'s unconfigured-server guard and got a `503 NOT_CONFIGURED` instead of logging in. Fixed by adding `FOND_STAFF_CODE: ci-test-code` and `FOND_DB_PATH: ':memory:'` to that step's `env:` block. Verified locally with the identical env vars before pushing (all 4 e2e tests passed), then pushed the fix (commit `Fix CI: set FOND_STAFF_CODE/FOND_DB_PATH for the e2e test:e2e step`). Confirmed both `verify` check runs on the PR's head commit now report `conclusion: success`.

Real header image: Brett confirmed the menu should match the two uploaded PDFs (it does — see the pivot entry above) and asked for a real header/hero photo from the "Midpoint Hub" folder he'd referenced earlier. That folder wasn't in this session's initially-connected device folders (only an unrelated `midpoint` project folder was); requested and was granted access to the actual OneDrive `Midpoint Hub` folder on Brett's Windows desktop, found 21 real venue/food photos there, and selected an exterior shot clearly showing "FOND EATERY" and "Midpoint HUB" signage (`WhatsApp Image 2026-09-14 at 11.14.48.jpeg`) as the best header candidate. Optimized it (ImageMagick: resized to 1600px wide, quality 78, metadata stripped, 244,170 bytes final) and pushed the two code changes it needs — `src/components/ordering-app.tsx` (hero `<img>` markup) and `src/app/globals.css` (`.hero-art` restyled for a real photo background with a dark Ink gradient overlay, `.hero-art::after`, plus a mobile min-height bump) — both plain-text changes, pushed successfully via the GitHub MCP tools.

**Tool limitation discovered**: the `mcp__remote-devices__github__push_files`/`create_or_update_file` MCP tools cannot upload true binary files. Verified experimentally: pushed a base64-encoded 1×1 PNG string as `content` to a throwaway path — the committed file's size matched the *literal base64 text's character length*, not the decoded PNG's byte size, proving the tool re-encodes whatever string it's given as literal UTF-8 rather than decoding a supplied base64 payload back to binary. No lower-level git blob/tree-creation tool was found via `ToolSearch` to work around this. Cleaned up by deleting the throwaway test file from the branch. Consequence: the actual `fond-hero.jpg` binary is not yet in the repository — only the code referencing `/images/fond-hero.jpg` is pushed, so the header will 404/show broken until the JPEG itself is added. Delivered the optimized JPEG to Brett directly via file transfer in the chat and asked him to upload it himself to `public/images/fond-hero.jpg` on the `feature/kill-yoco-staff-tablet` branch via GitHub's web "Add file → Upload files" UI, since this session's toolset cannot commit it. **This is a durable constraint for any future binary-asset work on this repo from this session's toolset — plan around it (deliver binaries to Brett for manual upload, or find/request a different tool) rather than re-attempting the same push path.**

Testing: local build/e2e verification re-run after each of the two pushes above (CI-fix push, hero-image push), restoring `playwright.config.ts` to its clean committed form after each local run (a temporary `executablePath` override is needed locally in this sandbox due to a Playwright browser version mismatch, but was never committed). All checks green on PR #2 as of this entry.

Push: PR #2 open, CI green, not yet merged. Deployment: none yet. Live verification: none yet; pending the binary image upload (Brett, manual step) before the header can render correctly on the deployed build.

Open items carried forward: get `public/images/fond-hero.jpg` into the repo (blocked on Brett's manual upload, see above); once in, verify the header renders on the actual PR/CI build and update this file; merge PR #2 once reviewed; then deploy and do the usual live-verification pass; all open items from the pivot entry above remain open unchanged.

### 2026-09-16 item preparation estimates and isolated Yoco sandbox checkout

Implementation: added a preparation estimate to every menu record and exposed it in each admin menu-item editor. Existing published items receive a one-time research-derived starting estimate; later admin changes are preserved. ### 2026-09-16 final Yoco POS operating decision

Business decision: Brett approved the existing manual handoff as FOND's final operating model. The supported flow is `FOND customer order -> signed Yoco online-payment confirmation or pay-in-person status -> staff accept -> staff capture the products/modifiers in the restaurant Yoco POS -> Yoco POS sends the order slip to its configured kitchen printer -> staff record the Yoco receipt/order reference in FOND -> preparation and fulfilment continue in the FOND tablet queue`. FOND will not wait for or claim an automatic public API connection that creates restaurant orders in Yoco POS.

Status: the architecture/product decision and required FOND software workflow are complete. The deployed staff queue already enforces the human-confirmed POS-reference gate before preparation and retains the actor, timestamp and reference in the audit trail. Automatic Yoco POS order creation is removed from the development backlog and must not be described as an outstanding defect. The restaurant still needs a one-time launch walkthrough proving staff can capture a representative order with modifiers and that the already configured Yoco kitchen printer produces the correct slip; this is operational acceptance/training for the chosen manual process, not an integration-development dependency. No code, provider credential or payment configuration changed for this decision. No additional test or deployment was required. No Asana tracker is in scope.

### 2026-09-21 Twilio SMS order notifications

Implementation: added SMS as a separate transactional channel while leaving WhatsApp unchanged. Customer collection and delivery checkout can opt in to accepted and ready SMS updates when SMS is enabled. FOND stores SMS consent separately, creates durable accepted/ready jobs, sends through Twilio from the configured E.164 sender, and records Twilio message IDs and queued/sent/delivered/undelivered/failed states. Ready text distinguishes collection from delivery. Twilio status callbacks are authenticated with the provider signature before updating a job. Super admins can save the SMS Account SID/sender, enable SMS independently, send one controlled test without creating an order, inspect the SMS log, and retry explicit failures. The shared Twilio auth token remains encrypted in the existing credential vault. Defaults keep SMS disabled on both production and staging until explicitly configured.

Testing: `npm run typecheck` passed; 74/74 unit tests passed, including SMS configuration validation, exact Twilio request fields, concise accepted/ready copy, consent-separated durable jobs, unconfigured-provider behavior and signed callback validation; `npm run build` passed with both `/api/admin/twilio/test-sms` and `/api/webhooks/twilio/sms`. The complete Playwright suite passed 40/40 across desktop and mobile against a fresh isolated SQLite database. A focused provider-access retest passed 2/2 and confirmed the super-admin provider response exposes an empty SMS configuration and the public callback URL. An earlier browser run used the repository's accumulated local database and failed order creation at the pre-existing kitchen-capacity limit; the same suite passed completely against the required clean database, so this was test-data state rather than an application failure. Twilio network calls were mocked and no real SMS was sent during local validation.

### 2026-09-21 branded transactional email templates

Implementation: replaced FOND's raw one-line email bodies with compact, Outlook-safe table layouts using inline styles, a hidden preview line, a text-only fallback and no externally hosted images. The controlled test, six-digit verification and received/accepted/ready/completed order messages now share the FOND Midpoint brand header and accessible content hierarchy. Order emails include reference, status, fulfilment timing, destination, payment method, line items, modifiers and trusted stored total. Subjects now describe the customer action or order state, and the sender display name is `FOND Midpoint`. User-controlled values are HTML escaped before rendering.

Deliverability diagnosis: Resend's Insights report for the first live message marked every available check `Doing great`, including valid inherited DMARC, the verified sending subdomain, matching links, plain text, small body, non-`no-reply` sender and image rules. DNS readback also confirmed Resend DKIM and SPF. The first message's Outlook spam placement is therefore not evidence of failed authentication; the more likely remaining factors are a brand-new sending subdomain/sender reputation and mailbox-level trust. No DNS record was changed. Inbox placement cannot be guaranteed by application code, and Brett should mark the next legitimate message `Not junk` if Outlook still filters it.

Testing: all 76 unit tests pass with new assertions for the display sender, branded HTML, plain-text fallback, verification-code layout and actual order item/total content. `npm.cmd run typecheck`, the Next.js production build and `git diff --check` pass. The controlled test template was rendered to real HTML and visually inspected at 2560×1440 and 390×844: the centered desktop card and single-column mobile layout are readable, unclipped and preserve hierarchy and touch-safe CTA spacing.

### 2026-09-22 staging-to-production operational configuration consolidation

Implementation: added a guarded, manual GitHub Actions workflow and two one-time server scripts that export only operational configuration from isolated staging and import it into production through a direct container pipe. The package includes the complete editable menu, promotion image library, published/draft/previous customer content and message documents when present, email sender, Twilio SMS and Meta configuration, encrypted provider credentials that are valid across environments, and missing team accounts. Credentials are decrypted only inside the staging container and immediately re-encrypted with the distinct production vault key; their values are not written to workflow logs or repository files. Production orders, customers, accounts, sessions, payments, notification jobs and audit history are excluded. Existing production team accounts and 2FA are preserved to prevent an access lockout. Both SQLite databases are backed up before each consolidation attempt.

Production safeguards: staging Yoco test credentials/webhook and Twilio WhatsApp Sandbox configuration are never promoted as live configuration. The importer always forces `allowTestPayments=false`; online checkout is enabled only if production already has a live Yoco key and signing secret. WhatsApp is enabled only when production has a complete Meta connection or registered Twilio sender plus approved Content SIDs. SMS is enabled only when the copied Account SID, E.164 sender and encrypted Twilio token form a complete configuration. The importer requires both an explicit production target and exact `fond.mid-point.co.za` host guard.

Testing: the new integration test builds separate staging and production SQLite databases with different vault keys, exports/imports the operational package, and proves menu/image/document transfer, credential re-encryption, SMS enablement, preservation of the existing production super-admin and Yoco credential, rejection of staging sandbox WhatsApp, and forced disabling of test/online payments. `npm.cmd test` passed 81/81, `npm.cmd run typecheck`, `npm.cmd run build` and `git diff --check` passed.

Merge: this repository uses authorized direct-main promotion; no separate pull request or merge was used.

The first consolidation run `35700069386` created both backups but stopped before opening or mutating production because `FOND_PUBLIC_URL` was not exposed inside the container. The exact host was then passed explicitly while retaining the guard. Replacement Verify FOND run `35700206226`, staging deploy `35700386744`, production promotion `35700476190` and consolidation run `35700563991` all passed for `d11e968`. The successful import reported 162 stored menu rows, zero promotion images, four present operational documents, no new team accounts, and re-encrypted `email-api`, `twilio-auth-token` and `meta-webhook-verify` credentials. Its health check returned `status=ok` with live ordering and in-person payments.

### 2026-09-22 preselected customer email and SMS order updates

Implementation: collection and delivery checkout now show SMS, email and WhatsApp notification choices together. SMS is selected by default and becomes available when a contact number is present. ### 2026-09-22 reject duplicate Yoco POS references

Implementation: staff Yoco POS recording now checks the trimmed reference inside the existing immediate SQLite transaction before writing the order. If another order already holds the same reference, including a case-only variation such as `YOCO-123` versus `yoco-123`, the server rejects it and identifies the FOND order that already owns the reference. The attempted second order remains unchanged and receives no POS-recorded audit event. The immediate transaction serializes competing tablet writes, so two staff devices cannot successfully claim the same new reference concurrently. Existing production records are not rewritten and startup is not blocked by any historic test data.

Testing: `git diff --check`, TypeScript validation, all 82 unit tests and the Next.js production build passed. The unit test records one reference, rejects the same reference with different case and surrounding spaces on a second order, and proves the second order remains unrecorded. The first unit run correctly exposed two old analytics fixtures that deliberately reused one static fake reference; those fixtures were changed to order-specific references. The first full browser run then passed 39/42 and exposed the same fixed-reference collision between parallel desktop/mobile projects in three browser fixtures. After making legitimate fixtures order-specific while retaining the deliberate duplicate attempt, the complete replacement Playwright suite passed 42/42 across desktop and mobile against a fresh isolated SQLite database. No real order, Yoco record, payment or provider call was made.

### 2026-09-22 direct guest email entry at checkout

Implementation: removed the account-verification gate from transactional checkout email. Every collection and delivery customer now sees an `Email address` field and an enabled, preselected `Email me when my order is accepted and ready` choice. A signed-in account pre-fills its address, but signed-out and unverified customers can enter a different valid address directly. The order endpoint normalizes the selected address, rejects missing or invalid addresses when email updates remain selected, includes the address in idempotency matching, and creates received/accepted/ready/completed email jobs for any opted-in order. Turning email updates off creates no email job and a signed-out guest address is not retained by the order API. SMS remains preselected and becomes enabled after a contact number is entered; WhatsApp remains visibly greyed out while its provider is unavailable. Account verification remains available to prove account ownership but is no longer required for transactional order updates.

The retry fixture was updated, then the complete replacement Playwright suite passed 42/42 across desktop and mobile against a fresh isolated SQLite database. Browser coverage proves an anonymous customer can enter an address and place/track an order, an unverified signed-in customer is prefilled and enabled, Email and configured SMS start selected, and unavailable WhatsApp stays disabled. ### 2026-09-22 two-message customer email journey and ready-order upsell

Implementation: customer order email now follows one clear two-message journey: `Order received` when the order is created, followed by either `Ready for collection` or `Ready for delivery` when staff move it to ready. Staff acceptance, preparation and completion remain operational workflow stages, but acceptance and completion no longer trigger duplicate customer email. The checkout opt-in now says `Email me when my order is received and ready`. The received email retains the blue receipt treatment and tells the customer that FOND will email again when the order is ready. Ready emails use a visually distinct dark-green `ORDER READY` status panel, state the correct collection/delivery handoff, retain the order reference and receipt details, and alone include the configurable next-order promotional banner. Messaging Studio now exposes only received, ready-collection and ready-delivery customer email templates, explains the lifecycle, labels the promotion as a ready-email upsell, and previews the different ready treatment. Legacy stored default subjects, ready wording and banner copy are upgraded on read without overwriting customized values.

Testing: `git diff --check`, TypeScript validation, all 85 unit tests and the Next.js production build passed. Focused email tests prove the automatic policy returns events only for received and ready; accepted, preparing, completed and cancelled return no customer-email event. Template coverage proves delivery-ready wording, the dark ready treatment, HTML escaping, ready-only banner rendering, and legacy-default upgrade. The first Playwright run reused an old local test server whose database was already at kitchen capacity, causing order fixtures to be rejected; it was discarded. A replacement run against a fresh isolated SQLite database passed 42/42. After adding explicit Messaging Studio coverage, a focused desktop/mobile run passed 2/2 and the final complete replacement suite passed 44/44 across desktop and mobile. It proves the three active email choices, distinct ready preview, ready-only upsell, updated checkout wording and the existing order flows. Automated provider calls are mocked or unavailable; no real order, payment, email, SMS or WhatsApp message was created.

### 2026-09-22 delivery dispatch tracking, friendly order numbers and tablet search

Implementation: corrected the customer tracking model so fulfilment and stage control the wording. A collection order at `ready` shows `Ready for collection` without a stale kitchen estimate. A delivery order at `ready` shows `Ready for delivery — waiting for dispatch`; staff must use the new `Send out for delivery` action before the customer sees `Out for delivery`. The out-for-delivery view shows an expected arrival time calculated from the configured delivery target and the actual dispatch timestamp, and delivery cannot be marked complete before dispatch. Existing received/ready email and SMS policy remains unchanged, so dispatch does not create a third or duplicate notification.

Every order now receives a unique customer-facing number in the form `FOND-7K3P-9Q8R`. The original 32-character UUID reference remains the internal Yoco, checkout and webhook identifier. Existing database orders are backfilled safely at startup and the new customer number has a unique index. Customer confirmation, tracking, My account, transactional email, SMS/WhatsApp payloads, staff cards, browser alerts, duplicate-Yoco errors and Admin order rows use the friendly number. Tracking accepts either the friendly number or the legacy internal reference for backward compatibility.

The authenticated staff tablet now has a prominent `Find any order` search across current and historic records. Staff can search by friendly or internal order number, customer name, mobile number, company, building or Yoco POS reference, then see fulfilment, status, contact/destination, total, items and the permanent audit trail. The board has a separate `Out for delivery` lane and preserves payment and POS controls.

Testing: `git diff --check`, TypeScript validation, all 86 unit tests and the Next.js production build passed. Unit coverage proves friendly-number creation/lookup, delivery dispatch sequencing, collection protection, historic search and the seventh queue lane. The first Playwright run used the repository's accumulated local database and correctly hit the configured kitchen-capacity guard; Playwright is now pinned to a fresh in-memory SQLite database so browser evidence cannot be polluted by prior test orders. The final complete Playwright suite passed 46/46 across desktop and mobile. It proves customer out-for-delivery/ETA wording excludes collection and preparation copy, friendly-number public lookup, authenticated staff history search, and the existing full staff POS/payment lifecycle. Automated tests made no provider call or production order.

### 2026-09-30 scan-first coffee reward redemption

Implementation: customer free-coffee dialogs now display a large scannable QR containing the existing reward code; manual code is tucked behind Show code instead. Staff Redeem coffee has a prominent Scan coffee reward button above the menu. The tablet requests the rear camera, decodes QR frames locally using jsQR, fills the reward code, and validates it automatically once a coffee is selected. Scanning before selection is supported. Membership QRs and other values are rejected. Camera tracks stop on a successful scan, Stop, drawer close/unmount, errors, or delayed permission resolution after close. A clear permission-recovery message and code-entry/USB scanner fallback remain available. Frames are not uploaded. Server-side verified ownership, environment matching, reservation, payment/collection rules and one-time redemption remain unchanged; no historical codes are replaced or shortened.

Testing: TypeScript validation and production build passed. QR and existing rewards-screen browser checks passed 12/12 across desktop/mobile; tablet layering checks also passed 2/2. Final scan-first-only QR checks passed 6/6. Tests decode the customer-rendered QR pixels to the exact fixture code, feed the QR into a simulated camera without BarcodeDetector support, prove automatic server validation after selecting the coffee, reject membership QR, verify camera track cleanup, and exercise permission denial and retained manual fallback. Existing browser coverage verifies revoked/redeemed reward displays and staff code/quote invalidation and order submission. Customer-phone and tablet screenshots were visually inspected. Camera input was simulated; physical tablet-to-phone scanning, lighting, focus and permission UAT remain unverified.
